The root is the same as \"apprehend.\""},{"backrefs":[{"href":"004-self.html#term-004-self-2","label":"4","podNum":"4"}],"definition":"Whitehead's term for the way each moment of experience actively feels and inherits the preceding moment."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"007-vacuum.html#term-007-vacuum-4","label":"7","podNum":"7"}],"definition":"the way one occasion takes in or \"feels\" another; the field is the medium through which occasions prehend each other."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"008-fields.html","label":"8","podNum":"8"}],"definition":"Alfred North Whitehead's term for the fundamental act by which any event takes in and is shaped by what surrounds it. Not a metaphor for perception but a proposed feature of reality at every scale: each occasion reaches out and feels its environment before it can become itself. Whitehead argued the electromagnetic field is the physical medium through which this reaching occurs."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"013-enactment.html#term-013-enactment-2","label":"13","podNum":"13"}],"definition":"Whitehead's term for how an occasion takes in its context; enactivism describes prehension at the scale of the organism."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"016-neuralnetwork.html#term-016-neuralnetwork-3","label":"16","podNum":"16"}],"definition":"the way occasions take in their past; the network's \"taking in\" of weights and activations is prehension."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"017-pastpresent.html#term-017-pastpresent-3","label":"17","podNum":"17"}],"definition":"For Whitehead, each occasion takes in its entire relevant past as fully present; the transformer's attention is a technical analogue."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"028-network.html#term-028-network-6","label":"28","podNum":"28"}],"definition":"Whitehead's term for the way each event takes in and responds to prior events. The basic relational act of reality."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"050-orthogonal.html#term-050-orthogonal-2","label":"50","podNum":"50"}],"definition":"Whitehead's term for how occasions take in their world; the interior dimension of process."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"080-unification.html#term-080-unification-3","label":"80","podNum":"80"}],"definition":"Whitehead's term for the process by which each actual occasion grasps, feels, and incorporates prior occasions into a new unity, the mechanism by which the many become one."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"prehension","term":"Prehension"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"003-inside.html#term-003-inside-4","label":"3","podNum":"3"}],"definition":"the philosophical position that experience or mind is a fundamental and ubiquitous feature of reality, not a late biological product. Distinguished from animism (which attributes spirits or agency to natural objects) by its grounding in rigorous argument rather than cultural tradition."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"028-network.html#term-028-network-7","label":"28","podNum":"28"}],"definition":"the philosophical position that experience or something like it is a fundamental and ubiquitous feature of reality, not a late biological emergence. Distinguished from animism by its philosophical rigor; from mysticism by its grounding in physics and mathematics."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"","url":""}],"slug":"panpsychism","term":"Panpsychism"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"003-inside.html","label":"3","podNum":"3"}],"definition":"David Chalmers' term (1995) for the question of why any physical process gives rise to subjective experience at all. Distinguished from the \"easy problems\" of explaining attention, memory, and behavior, which are hard in practice but tractable in principle. The hard problem is why there is something it is like to be a system at all."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"","url":""}],"slug":"hard-problem-of-consciousness","term":"Hard problem of consciousness"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"004-self.html#term-004-self-1","label":"4","podNum":"4"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"self","term":"self"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"004-self.html#term-004-self-3","label":"4","podNum":"4"}],"definition":"the religious and philosophical tradition founded on the teachings of Siddhartha Gautama (c. 5th century BCE). For this pod's argument, the relevant doctrine is anatta (no-self): what we call \"I\" is a process, not a thing, with no permanent substrate persisting through the changes."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"buddhism","term":"buddhism"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"004-self.html","label":"4","podNum":"4"}],"definition":"The Buddhist doctrine of no fixed self; experience arises moment to moment with no underlying owner."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"anatta","term":"Anatta"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"004-self.html#term-004-self-5","label":"4","podNum":"4"}],"definition":"Not a fact about the world but a convention we impose on a gradient. What survives across time (memories, intentions, characteristics) comes in degrees; the sharp line between \"you\" and \"someone else\" is something we draw, not something we find. Derek Parfit's term of art, congruent with the Buddhist doctrine of anatta and Whitehead's society of actual occasions."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"identity","term":"identity"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"005-freewill.html#term-005-freewill-1","label":"5","podNum":"5"}],"definition":"the doctrine that every event is fully fixed by prior causes and the laws of nature, so that given the past in full detail there is exactly one possible future. Treats apparent novelty as either illusion or noise."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"determinism","term":"determinism"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"005-freewill.html#term-005-freewill-2","label":"5","podNum":"5"}],"definition":"the capacity of an agent to make a genuine choice that is neither fully determined by prior causes nor merely random. In Whitehead's framing, this is not a special property of minds but the basic character of every occasion's self-determination."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"free-will","term":"free-will"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"005-freewill.html#term-005-freewill-3","label":"5","podNum":"5"}],"definition":"In process philosophy, each occasion's moment of decision that cannot be fully predicted from its past."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"self-determination","term":"self-determination"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"005-freewill.html#term-005-freewill-4","label":"5","podNum":"5"}],"definition":"Whitehead's term for the irreducible novelty introduced by each occasion as it resolves possibility into actuality."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"058-galacticnets.html#term-058-galacticnets-2","label":"58","podNum":"58"}],"definition":"Whitehead's term for the universe's ongoing production of novelty; here applied across scales."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"creative-advance","term":"Creative advance"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"005-freewill.html#term-005-freewill-5","label":"5","podNum":"5"}],"definition":"in Whitehead's metaphysics, the universal of universals: the principle by which many things become one and are increased by one. The genuine production of novelty at the heart of each occasion, present at every scale where possibility resolves into actuality."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"creativity","term":"creativity"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"006-output.html#term-006-output-1","label":"6","podNum":"6"}],"definition":"used here in the broadest sense: any process that selects a next state given a current one. Deliberately spans thermostats and minds."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"prediction","term":"prediction"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"006-output.html#term-006-output-2","label":"6","podNum":"6"},{"href":"036-universenoticing.html#term-036-universenoticing-1","label":"36","podNum":"36"}],"definition":"in Whitehead's sense, the actual unfolding activity of an occasion of experience. Distinct from its products or traces."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"process","term":"Process"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"006-output.html#term-006-output-3","label":"6","podNum":"6"}],"definition":"the channel through which a process expresses itself externally. The interface constrains and shapes what can be expressed; it does not determine what is being expressed."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"No short standalone explainer found for human output channel \/ expression vs content; concept appears in academic and medium-shape-message literature not as a short video.","url":""}],"slug":"output-interface","term":"Output interface"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"006-output.html#term-006-output-4","label":"6","podNum":"6"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"intelligence","term":"intelligence"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"007-vacuum.html#term-007-vacuum-1","label":"7","podNum":"7"}],"definition":"In QFT, short-lived excitations of the vacuum that contribute to processes; not \"things\" but possibilities being sampled."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"virtual-particle","term":"Virtual particle"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"007-vacuum.html#term-007-vacuum-2","label":"7","podNum":"7"}],"definition":"Whitehead's pure possibilities that occasions select from and realize; the quantum vacuum's virtual states are a physical analogue."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"066-cosmicattractor.html#term-066-cosmicattractor-1","label":"66","podNum":"66"}],"definition":"the realm of possibility that God holds available; occasions select from it in their self-determination."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"eternal-objects","term":"Eternal objects"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"007-vacuum.html#term-007-vacuum-3","label":"7","podNum":"7"},{"href":"008-fields.html#term-008-fields-3","label":"8","podNum":"8"}],"definition":"in physics, an entity that has a value at every point in space. Not a stage on which events occur but the very medium through which events reach out and feel each other. The electromagnetic field, the quantum vacuum, the weight space of a neural network are all fields in this sense."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"016-neuralnetwork.html#term-016-neuralnetwork-4","label":"16","podNum":"16"}],"definition":"in a neural network, the weight space, the high-dimensional manifold of all possible parameter configurations from which the forward pass selects a particular realization. The same structural role that the quantum vacuum plays in physics: not a stage but a medium of possibility."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"029-lighttime.html#term-029-lighttime-7","label":"29","podNum":"29"}],"definition":"in quantum field theory, the fundamental entity from which particles arise as localized excitations. The electromagnetic field pervades all space and is the medium through which charged particles interact. A photon is not a thing traveling through the field; it is a quantum of the field itself."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"030-interference.html#term-030-interference-3","label":"30","podNum":"30"}],"definition":"the unified Maxwell field that pervades all space, of which electricity, magnetism, and light are aspects. The medium through which charged particles, including neurons, interact and through which their disturbances propagate as overlapping waves."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"fields","term":"fields"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"007-vacuum.html#term-007-vacuum-5","label":"7","podNum":"7"}],"definition":"Prigogine's term (Nobel Prize, 1977) for ordered patterns that arise and persist in systems far from thermodynamic equilibrium, sustained by a continuous through-flow of energy. The biological cell is one instance; convection cells and chemical oscillators are others. The bridge between thermodynamics and self-organization."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"dissipative-structure","term":"Dissipative structure"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"008-fields.html#term-008-fields-2","label":"8","podNum":"8"},{"href":"009-randomness.html#term-009-randomness-1","label":"9","podNum":"9"},{"href":"028-network.html#term-028-network-2","label":"28","podNum":"28"},{"href":"063-mandelcrystals.html#term-063-mandelcrystals-3","label":"63","podNum":"63"},{"href":"072-temperature.html","label":"72","podNum":"72"}],"definition":"the discipline that tracks what is actually there at the most fundamental level we can currently measure. Not a fixed picture of reality but the live edge of what description can be made to hold; what counts as physics has changed every century and will change again."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"physics","term":"physics"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"008-fields.html#term-008-fields-4","label":"8","podNum":"8"}],"definition":"the unified field described by James Clerk Maxwell's equations (1865), of which electricity, magnetism, and light are all aspects. Not a medium that contains these phenomena but the phenomenon itself. Pervades all of space."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"Science Asylum — 9:00","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=UzW_jAJzlgI"},{"notes":"Crash Course Physics — 9:00","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=K40lNL3KsJ4"}],"slug":"electromagnetic-field","term":"Electromagnetic field"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"008-fields.html","label":"8","podNum":"8"}],"definition":"the branch of physics that unifies electricity, magnetism, and light as aspects of a single field, formulated mathematically by Maxwell in 1865."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"electromagnetism","term":"electromagnetism"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"008-fields.html#term-008-fields-5","label":"8","podNum":"8"}],"definition":"what the electromagnetic field does when a charged particle accelerates. The disturbance propagates outward as light. A stationary charge is silent; only acceleration speaks."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"radiation","term":"Radiation"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"008-fields.html#term-008-fields-6","label":"8","podNum":"8"}],"definition":"the spreading and interference of waves when they pass through a gap or around an obstacle. Visible when light passes through a mesh screen. Evidence that light is a field phenomenon, not a particle traveling a straight path."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"Diffraction of light — 12:24","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=X5xCBH7IIX0"},{"notes":"Khan Academy — 14:00","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=VmN3i4HW5l0"}],"slug":"diffraction","term":"Diffraction"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"009-randomness.html#term-009-randomness-2","label":"9","podNum":"9"}],"definition":"the process by which a system in a perfectly symmetric but unstable state falls into an asymmetric lower-energy state through random perturbation, and stays there. The mechanism behind crystal formation, dune building, and the large-scale structure of the universe."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"","url":""}],"slug":"spontaneous-symmetry-breaking","term":"Spontaneous symmetry breaking"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"009-randomness.html#term-009-randomness-3","label":"9","podNum":"9"},{"href":"012-autopoiesis.html#term-012-autopoiesis-5","label":"12","podNum":"12"},{"href":"032-skyfossil.html#term-032-skyfossil-1","label":"32","podNum":"32"},{"href":"041-colorwheel.html#term-041-colorwheel-5","label":"41","podNum":"41"},{"href":"044-ai-retina.html","label":"44","podNum":"44"},{"href":"045-baldwin.html#term-045-baldwin-1","label":"45","podNum":"45"},{"href":"055-patience.html#term-055-patience-1","label":"55","podNum":"55"},{"href":"055-patience.html#term-055-patience-2","label":"55","podNum":"55"},{"href":"059-galaxyevolve.html#term-059-galaxyevolve-1","label":"59","podNum":"59"}],"definition":"the process by which random variation passes through a non-random filter to produce structure that looks designed but isn't. Mutation supplies the noise, natural selection supplies the rule, and what accumulates across enough generations is an eye, a wing, an immune system. The intuition that randomness alone cannot build complexity is correct; the missed beat is that randomness was never alone."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"evolution","term":"evolution"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"009-randomness.html#term-009-randomness-4","label":"9","podNum":"9"},{"href":"011-moreisdifferent.html#term-011-moreisdifferent-2","label":"11","podNum":"11"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"complexity","term":"complexity"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"009-randomness.html#term-009-randomness-5","label":"9","podNum":"9"}],"definition":"a network of molecules in which each member's production is catalyzed by other members of the set. Stuart Kauffman's demonstration that chemistry at sufficient complexity spontaneously organizes itself without external direction."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"","url":""}],"slug":"autocatalytic-set","term":"Autocatalytic set"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"009-randomness.html#term-009-randomness-6","label":"9","podNum":"9"},{"href":"055-patience.html#term-055-patience-5","label":"55","podNum":"55"}],"definition":"the property of living systems that continuously produce the very components that sustain them; coined by Maturana and Varela. The cell does not merely use its components but makes them, circularly, as the condition of its own continued existence."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"012-autopoiesis.html#term-012-autopoiesis-3","label":"12","podNum":"12"}],"definition":"Maturana and Varela's term for a system that continuously produces the very components that constitute and maintain it. The defining organizational property of living systems."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"","url":""}],"slug":"autopoiesis","term":"Autopoiesis"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"009-randomness.html#term-009-randomness-7","label":"9","podNum":"9"}],"definition":"a solution that is better than all its immediate neighbors but not the best solution overall. The trap that pure hill-climbing optimization falls into; escaped by introducing randomness into the search."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"","url":""}],"slug":"local-maximum","term":"Local maximum"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"009-randomness.html#term-009-randomness-8","label":"9","podNum":"9"}],"definition":"an optimization technique borrowed from metallurgy that uses controlled randomness to escape local maxima. By occasionally accepting worse solutions, the algorithm avoids getting trapped and finds better global solutions. Used in engineering, chip design, and AI training."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"","url":""}],"slug":"simulated-annealing","term":"Simulated annealing"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"009-randomness.html#term-009-randomness-9","label":"9","podNum":"9"}],"definition":"the training algorithm used in neural networks that deliberately injects randomness into each optimization step. Pure deterministic optimization gets trapped in local minima; the randomness keeps the search moving toward better solutions."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"","url":""}],"slug":"stochastic-gradient-descent","term":"Stochastic gradient descent"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"009-randomness.html#term-009-randomness-10","label":"9","podNum":"9"}],"definition":"Whitehead's term for the irreducible self-determination at the heart of each actual occasion; the source of genuine newness in the universe that cannot be fully predicted from prior causes."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"","url":""}],"slug":"creative-advance-into-novelty","term":"Creative advance into novelty"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"020-penrose.html#term-020-penrose-1","label":"20","podNum":"20"},{"href":"010-largenumbers.html#term-010-largenumbers-1","label":"10","podNum":"10"}],"definition":"Kurt Gödel's 1931 proof that any consistent formal system powerful enough to describe arithmetic contains true statements it cannot prove from within itself."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"incompleteness-theorem","term":"incompleteness theorem"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"010-largenumbers.html#term-010-largenumbers-2","label":"10","podNum":"10"},{"href":"011-moreisdifferent.html#term-011-moreisdifferent-6","label":"11","podNum":"11"},{"href":"020-penrose.html#term-020-penrose-3","label":"20","podNum":"20"},{"href":"051-spacetime.html","label":"51","podNum":"51"}],"definition":"the appearance of qualitatively new properties or capacities when simpler processes accumulate or nest beyond a threshold."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"emergence","term":"emergence"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"011-moreisdifferent.html#term-011-moreisdifferent-1","label":"11","podNum":"11"}],"definition":"Philip Anderson's phrase (1972) for the principle that each level of complexity has emergent properties that cannot be derived from the level below. Not a violation of lower-level laws, but genuinely new phenomena requiring their own description."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"","url":""}],"slug":"more-is-different","term":"more is different"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"011-moreisdifferent.html#term-011-moreisdifferent-4","label":"11","podNum":"11"}],"definition":"Arthur Koestler's term for any entity that is simultaneously a whole in its own right and a part of a larger whole. Reality in Koestler's picture is a nested hierarchy of holons rather than parts assembling into wholes."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"024-ghostmachine.html#term-024-ghostmachine-2","label":"24","podNum":"24"}],"definition":"Koestler's term for a whole that is also a part of a larger whole; each level has genuine interiority."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"037-assembly.html#term-037-assembly-1","label":"37","podNum":"37"}],"definition":"Koestler's whole\/part; the stable \"sentence\" at one level becomes the \"word\" at the next."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"","url":""}],"slug":"holon","term":"Holon"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"011-moreisdifferent.html#term-011-moreisdifferent-5","label":"11","podNum":"11"}],"definition":"Koestler's term for the collision of two independent frames of reference that releases energy as laughter, insight, or aesthetic emotion. His claim: this is the common structure of humor, scientific discovery, and artistic creation."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"","url":""}],"slug":"bisociation","term":"Bisociation"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"011-moreisdifferent.html#term-011-moreisdifferent-7","label":"11","podNum":"11"}],"definition":"Whitehead's term for a stable pattern of events organized around a common characteristic that persists through time. Ant colonies, cities, brains, and neural networks are all societies in this sense."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"","url":""}],"slug":"society-of-actual-occasions","term":"Society of actual occasions"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"012-autopoiesis.html#term-012-autopoiesis-1","label":"12","podNum":"12"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"life","term":"life"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"012-autopoiesis.html#term-012-autopoiesis-2","label":"12","podNum":"12"}],"definition":"the smallest unit of life that maintains itself: a membrane-bounded chemical system that continuously produces the very molecules its existence depends on. All living things are made of cells; the simplest, bacteria, are themselves living."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"cells","term":"cells"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"012-autopoiesis.html#term-012-autopoiesis-4","label":"12","podNum":"12"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"entropy","term":"entropy"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"012-autopoiesis.html","label":"12","podNum":"12"}],"definition":"Whitehead's term for a persistent pattern of actual occasions organized with sufficient coherence and closure to act as a unit through time. The metaphysical counterpart to the autopoietic organization."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"014-cities.html#term-014-cities-1","label":"14","podNum":"14"}],"definition":"Whitehead's term for a stable pattern of events with a common character; a city is a society of human occasions."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"037-assembly.html#term-037-assembly-2","label":"37","podNum":"37"}],"definition":"Whitehead's persistent pattern of events that functions as vocabulary for the next level."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"","url":""}],"slug":"society-of-occasions","term":"society of occasions"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"012-autopoiesis.html#term-012-autopoiesis-6","label":"12","podNum":"12"}],"definition":"Schrödinger's term for the ordered structure that living systems import from their environment to offset internal disorder. The thermodynamic face of what autopoiesis achieves organizationally."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"","url":""}],"slug":"negative-entropy","term":"negative entropy"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"013-enactment.html","label":"13","podNum":"13"}],"definition":"The view that cognition is not representation of a pregiven world but the enactment of a world through embodied action."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"enactivism","term":"Enactivism"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"013-enactment.html#term-013-enactment-1","label":"13","podNum":"13"},{"href":"022-locked.html#term-022-locked-6","label":"22","podNum":"22"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"cognition","term":"cognition"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"015-aliens.html#term-015-aliens-1","label":"15","podNum":"15"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"culture","term":"culture"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"016-neuralnetwork.html#term-016-neuralnetwork-1","label":"16","podNum":"16"},{"href":"053-dimensions.html#term-053-dimensions-3","label":"53","podNum":"53"},{"href":"056-mandelneural.html","label":"56","podNum":"56"},{"href":"057-superfarey.html","label":"57","podNum":"57"}],"definition":"a layered system of artificial neurons whose weighted connections are adjusted by training on data. The dominant substrate of modern AI. Loosely inspired by biological neurons but architecturally and operationally distinct; the resemblance is more analogical than mechanistic."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"neural-networks","term":"neural-networks"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"017-pastpresent.html#term-017-pastpresent-1","label":"17","podNum":"17"}],"definition":"the neural architecture that holds full context in parallel and attends over it; time becomes spatial in the representation."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"transformer","term":"Transformer"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"017-pastpresent.html#term-017-pastpresent-4","label":"17","podNum":"17"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"memory","term":"memory"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"018-magnetization.html#term-018-magnetization-1","label":"18","podNum":"18"}],"definition":"a change in the state of a system, liquid to solid, disordered to ordered, uncommitted to aligned. Requires the medium to be in the right condition (cooled, supersaturated, primed) and seeds to initiate the shift."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"phase-transition","term":"phase transition"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"018-magnetization.html#term-018-magnetization-2","label":"18","podNum":"18"}],"definition":"a region within a ferromagnetic material where atomic spins are aligned. Domains grow by recruiting neighbors at their boundaries until the whole material is magnetized."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"magnetic-domain","term":"magnetic domain"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"018-magnetization.html#term-018-magnetization-3","label":"18","podNum":"18"}],"definition":"the formation of small ordered regions (nuclei or seeds) within a disordered medium. The seeds provide a template that neighboring elements can join, initiating a phase transition."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"nucleation","term":"nucleation"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"018-magnetization.html#term-018-magnetization-4","label":"18","podNum":"18"}],"definition":"the critical moment when isolated clusters become a connected network. Before it, seeds are islands; after it, they form a continent and the remaining disorder is surrounded."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"percolation-threshold","term":"percolation threshold"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"019-funerals.html","label":"19","podNum":"19"}],"definition":"Kuhn's term for the framework of assumptions and practices that defines normal science until crisis and revolution."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"027-usedtothink.html#term-027-usedtothink-4","label":"27","podNum":"27"}],"definition":"Thomas Kuhn's term for the framework of assumptions, methods, and exemplary problems that defines normal science in a given period. The paradigm is not a theory, it is the background against which theories are tested. Scientists inside a paradigm generally cannot see it as a paradigm; it simply looks like reality."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"Thomas Kuhn: Structure of Scientific Revolutions — 14:31","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=L70T4pQv7P8"},{"notes":"paradigm shift — 10:00","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=tJD5RMpIw18"},{"notes":"What is a Paradigm? Kuhn and Normal Science — 50:30","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=7gI25jArTgo"}],"slug":"paradigm","term":"Paradigm"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"019-funerals.html#term-019-funerals-1","label":"19","podNum":"19"}],"definition":"the mechanism by which new paradigms prevail, not by convincing holdouts but by outlasting them."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"generational-replacement","term":"generational replacement"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"020-penrose.html#term-020-penrose-2","label":"20","podNum":"20"}],"definition":"Penrose and Hameroff's proposed quantum-gravitational mechanism for consciousness, situated in neuronal microtubules and collapsed by objective reduction. Speculative; the most detailed attempt to ground consciousness in quantum mechanics."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"orch-or","term":"Orch-OR"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"021-just.html#term-021-just-1","label":"21","podNum":"21"}],"definition":"Ryle's term for applying an explanation from one category to something in a different category, then treating it as complete. The Concept of Mind (1949)."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"022-locked.html#term-022-locked-8","label":"22","podNum":"22"}],"definition":"Ryle's term for applying a description from one category as if it were a complete account of something in another. See seedpod 021."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"024-ghostmachine.html#term-024-ghostmachine-1","label":"24","podNum":"24"}],"definition":"Ryle's term for treating a process or capacity as if it were a thing (e.g. the mind as a ghost in the machine)."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"Jeffrey Kaplan — 16:00","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=gA-2Gc3PztI"},{"notes":"Untangle Philosophy — 6:00","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=VaQK-XHEG-g"}],"slug":"category-mistake","term":"category mistake"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"021-just.html#term-021-just-3","label":"21","podNum":"21"}],"definition":"Dennett's term for a device that makes a conclusion feel obvious without establishing it through argument. Intuition Pumps and Other Tools for Thinking (2013)."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"Talks at Google \/ Dennett — 62:00","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=4Q_mY54hjM0"},{"notes":"Big Think \/ Dennett — 3:00","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=5tvT90uPz-U"}],"slug":"intuition-pump","term":"intuition pump"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"021-just.html#term-021-just-4","label":"21","podNum":"21"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"dismissal","term":"dismissal"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"022-locked.html#term-022-locked-1","label":"22","podNum":"22"}],"definition":"a condition of wakefulness without awareness, in which the eyes may be open and basic reflexes present but conscious experience is absent or undetectable. Diagnosis is behavioral and therefore vulnerable to the error this seedpod describes."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"","url":""}],"slug":"vegetative-state","term":"vegetative state"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"022-locked.html#term-022-locked-2","label":"22","podNum":"22"}],"definition":"the means of output through which an interior communicates with the world: speech, movement, gesture, even a blink. The channel can be wide or narrow, familiar or unfamiliar, present or absent. Its width says nothing about what feeds it."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"channel","term":"channel"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"022-locked.html#term-022-locked-3","label":"22","podNum":"22"}],"definition":"the source that feeds the channel. The interior: cognition, experience, awareness. What is actually there, regardless of whether it can be seen."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"Explain It! Presuming Competence — 3:45","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=Elm2FiUoGso"},{"notes":"Presuming Competence — 4:48","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=w70VNAN4HfM"}],"slug":"capacity","term":"capacity"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"022-locked.html#term-022-locked-4","label":"22","podNum":"22"}],"definition":"a neurological condition in which a person is fully conscious and cognitively intact but almost completely paralyzed, typically retaining only eye movement. Caused by damage to the brainstem. Distinguished from vegetative state, in which consciousness itself is absent or severely impaired."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"Locked In My Body — 14:08","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=0k8sKX6IeMs"},{"notes":"Locked-In Syndrome: Trapped Inside Your Own Body — 4:43","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=NVDkxpPKZPI"}],"slug":"locked-in-syndrome","term":"locked-in syndrome"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"022-locked.html#term-022-locked-5","label":"22","podNum":"22"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"autism","term":"autism"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"022-locked.html#term-022-locked-7","label":"22","podNum":"22"}],"definition":"a principle in disability advocacy and education: treat cognitive capacity as present until there is genuine evidence otherwise, rather than assuming absence because familiar output is missing."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"Explain It! Presuming Competence — 3:45","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=Elm2FiUoGso"},{"notes":"Presuming Competence — 4:48","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=w70VNAN4HfM"}],"slug":"presume-competence","term":"presume competence"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"025-widechannel.html#term-025-widechannel-1","label":"25","podNum":"25"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"output","term":"output"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"026-twotruths.html#term-026-twotruths-1","label":"26","podNum":"26"}],"definition":"a set of assumptions, categories, and relationships that organize how we interpret experience. Neither alone is sufficient; both together are required."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"complementarity","term":"complementarity"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"027-usedtothink.html#term-027-usedtothink-1","label":"27","podNum":"27"},{"href":"047-ai-feeling.html","label":"47","podNum":"47"}],"definition":"the 18th-century theory that heat is a subtle fluid. Developed by Lavoisier; displaced by kinetic theory. Not a failure of intelligence, a triumph of science eventually superseded."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"First Law of Thermodynamics: History of the Concept of Energy — 22:42","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=a9c7u-FM-Wc"},{"notes":"AMS OpenMind \/ Lavoisier — 2:53","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=55yJPs-_R1w"}],"slug":"caloric","term":"caloric"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"027-usedtothink.html#term-027-usedtothink-2","label":"27","podNum":"27"}],"definition":"the attribution of experience, agency, or spirit to natural phenomena. Dismissed by the materialist framework as projection or superstition. The dismissal may have been overconfident about the phenomenon, not just the mechanism."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"Animism: The First Religion? — 8:57","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=xoYZ5xNyKbc"}],"slug":"animism","term":"animism"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"027-usedtothink.html#term-027-usedtothink-3","label":"27","podNum":"27"}],"definition":"the doctrine that living organisms contain a special life-force absent from non-living matter. Dismissed when mechanistic biology showed chemical processes suffice. The dismissal was correct about the mechanism; the phenomenon it was tracking, that living organization is qualitatively distinct, was real."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"Is Vitalism Making a Comeback? — 33:09","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=nvszjQCT1do"}],"slug":"vitalism","term":"vitalism"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"027-usedtothink.html#term-027-usedtothink-5","label":"27","podNum":"27"}],"definition":"the tendency to narrate the history of science as a story of steady progress toward current knowledge, reading the past as a series of steps toward us. Named by Herbert Butterfield (1931). \"We used to think \/ now we know\" is its verbal formula."}],"explainers":[{"notes":"origins of modern science \/ Gal — 38:24","url":"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/watch?v=PxKucDmj4VY"}],"slug":"whig-history-of-science","term":"whig history of science"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"028-network.html#term-028-network-1","label":"28","podNum":"28"},{"href":"029-lighttime.html#term-029-lighttime-1","label":"29","podNum":"29"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"relativity","term":"relativity"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"028-network.html#term-028-network-3","label":"28","podNum":"28"}],"definition":"the perspective from which measurements are made. Different reference frames can disagree about durations and distances while agreeing on the underlying physics. The photon's reference frame is a legitimate physical perspective."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"reference-frame","term":"reference frame"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"028-network.html#term-028-network-4","label":"28","podNum":"28"}],"definition":"in special relativity, the time elapsed as measured by a clock moving with an object. For a photon moving at the speed of light, proper time is zero. The photon does not experience duration."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"029-lighttime.html#term-029-lighttime-4","label":"29","podNum":"29"}],"definition":"in special relativity, the time elapsed as measured by a clock traveling with an object. For a photon traveling at the speed of light, proper time is zero, emission and absorption are the same event from the photon's perspective."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"proper-time","term":"proper time"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"028-network.html#term-028-network-5","label":"28","podNum":"28"}],"definition":"John Wheeler's 1940s proposal, conveyed to Richard Feynman, that a single electron tracing a worldline through spacetime, zigzagging forward and backward in time, could account for all electrons and positrons in the universe. Not a mainstream theory; notable as a pointer toward the possibility that apparent multiplicity may be underlying unity."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"wheeler-one-electron-universe","term":"wheeler one-electron universe"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"029-lighttime.html#term-029-lighttime-2","label":"29","podNum":"29"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"time","term":"time"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"029-lighttime.html#term-029-lighttime-3","label":"29","podNum":"29"},{"href":"069-love.html","label":"69","podNum":"69"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"light","term":"light"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"029-lighttime.html#term-029-lighttime-5","label":"29","podNum":"29"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"harmonics","term":"harmonics"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"029-lighttime.html#term-029-lighttime-6","label":"29","podNum":"29"}],"definition":"the set of frequencies naturally produced by a vibrating physical object, string, air column, membrane. Integer multiples of the fundamental frequency: 2x (octave), 3x (fifth plus octave), 4x (two octaves), and so on. The physical basis of musical consonance."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"overtone-series","term":"overtone series"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"030-interference.html#term-030-interference-1","label":"30","podNum":"30"},{"href":"038-consonance-map.html#term-038-consonance-map-2","label":"38","podNum":"38"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"music","term":"music"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"030-interference.html#term-030-interference-2","label":"30","podNum":"30"}],"definition":"Thomas Young's 1801 experiment in which light passing through two narrow slits produces an interference pattern of bands on a screen behind them. The pattern can only be explained if light spreads as a wave from each slit and the waves combine. Later extended to electrons, atoms, and even small molecules, showing that wave behavior is universal at the quantum scale."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"double-slit","term":"double-slit"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"030-interference.html#term-030-interference-4","label":"30","podNum":"30"}],"definition":"the experience of elements fitting together with a sense of inevitability, governed by simple ratios between their frequencies. In music: the overlapping wave patterns of consonant intervals. In thought: possibly the same wave-overlap structure expressed in neural firing rather than air pressure."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"070-harmony.html","label":"70","podNum":"70"}],"definition":"the experience of elements fitting together, in sound, in color, in social life, with a sense of inevitability governed by the simplicity of the ratios between them."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"harmony","term":"harmony"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"031-surfaceinside.html#term-031-surfaceinside-1","label":"31","podNum":"31"},{"href":"033-edge.html#term-033-edge-2","label":"33","podNum":"33"},{"href":"068-bubbles.html#term-068-bubbles-3","label":"68","podNum":"68"}],"definition":"The surface between inside and outside. In physics, where information lives. In Sufi metaphysics (barzakh), a mediating realm of its own."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"boundary","term":"boundary"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"031-surfaceinside.html#term-031-surfaceinside-2","label":"31","podNum":"31"}],"definition":"The principle that all information in a volume of space is encoded on its boundary surface, not distributed through the interior. Discovered by Bekenstein and developed through Hawking's work on black holes."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"holographic","term":"holographic"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"031-surfaceinside.html#term-031-surfaceinside-3","label":"31","podNum":"31"}],"definition":"In Sufi metaphysics, especially Ibn Arabi, the isthmus or intermediate realm that separates and connects different states of existence. The boundary where the invisible becomes visible."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"barzakh","term":"barzakh"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"031-surfaceinside.html#term-031-surfaceinside-4","label":"31","podNum":"31"},{"href":"068-bubbles.html#term-068-bubbles-2","label":"68","podNum":"68"}],"definition":"Whitehead's sense of \"inside\", not spatial but experiential. What an event is from within."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"interiority","term":"interiority"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"032-skyfossil.html#term-032-skyfossil-2","label":"32","podNum":"32"},{"href":"053-dimensions.html#term-053-dimensions-4","label":"53","podNum":"53"},{"href":"054-bornharmonic.html#term-054-bornharmonic-3","label":"54","podNum":"54"},{"href":"064-choicedimension.html","label":"64","podNum":"64"}],"definition":"Hugh Everett's 1957 interpretation of quantum mechanics in which the wavefunction never collapses. Every quantum measurement causes the universe to branch, with each possible outcome realized in a separate world. No special role for observers; no mysterious collapse mechanism. The cost is an explosion of unobservable branches; the benefit is a strictly unitary, deterministic theory."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"060-worldtests.html#term-060-worldtests-1","label":"60","podNum":"60"}],"definition":"Everett's interpretation in which all quantum branches are real; the wavefunction does not collapse."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"many-worlds","term":"many-worlds"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"032-skyfossil.html#term-032-skyfossil-3","label":"32","podNum":"32"},{"href":"036-universenoticing.html#term-036-universenoticing-2","label":"36","podNum":"36"},{"href":"055-patience.html#term-055-patience-4","label":"55","podNum":"55"}],"definition":"Pierre Teilhard de Chardin's term for the projected end-point of cosmic evolution: the universe becoming fully self-aware. From The Phenomenon of Man (1955). A directional claim about evolution, controversial in mainstream biology, that the arrow of complexity points somewhere."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"059-galaxyevolve.html#term-059-galaxyevolve-2","label":"59","podNum":"59"}],"definition":"Teilhard's convergence of cosmic evolution toward maximum complexity and self-awareness."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"omega-point","term":"omega-point"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"033-edge.html#term-033-edge-1","label":"33","podNum":"33"},{"href":"034-onerule.html#term-034-onerule-1","label":"34","podNum":"34"},{"href":"054-bornharmonic.html","label":"54","podNum":"54"},{"href":"056-mandelneural.html","label":"56","podNum":"56"},{"href":"062-branchingdice.html#term-062-branchingdice-1","label":"62","podNum":"62"},{"href":"063-mandelcrystals.html#term-063-mandelcrystals-1","label":"63","podNum":"63"},{"href":"064-choicedimension.html#term-064-choicedimension-1","label":"64","podNum":"64"}],"definition":"the set of points c in the complex plane for which the iteration z → z² + c, starting at z = 0, stays bounded forever. Plotted as black against escape-velocity colors, it produces an infinitely intricate fractal boundary that has become the iconic image of mathematical complexity arising from a single simple rule."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"mandelbrot-set","term":"mandelbrot-set"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"033-edge.html#term-033-edge-3","label":"33","podNum":"33"},{"href":"035-simplestnumbers.html#term-035-simplestnumbers-2","label":"35","podNum":"35"},{"href":"053-dimensions.html","label":"53","podNum":"53"},{"href":"057-superfarey.html","label":"57","podNum":"57"}],"definition":"the sequence of all fractions p\/q between 0 and 1 with denominator q ≤ N, listed in increasing order. As N grows, simpler fractions appear earlier; the structure of the sequence is the structure of rational complexity. Named for John Farey (1816), though the pattern was noticed earlier. Governs musical consonance, Mandelbrot bulb positions, and the orthogonality of high-dimensional concept directions in neural networks."},{"backrefs":[{"href":"038-consonance-map.html","label":"38","podNum":"38"}],"definition":"the set of all fractions between 0 and 1 ordered by complexity, simplest denominators first. Governs both the positions of bulbs on The Mandelbrot cardioid and the hierarchy of consonant intervals in music."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"farey-sequence","term":"farey-sequence"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"033-edge.html#term-033-edge-4","label":"33","podNum":"33"},{"href":"038-consonance-map.html#term-038-consonance-map-3","label":"38","podNum":"38"},{"href":"040-stability.html","label":"40","podNum":"40"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"consonance","term":"consonance"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"033-edge.html#term-033-edge-5","label":"33","podNum":"33"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"fractal","term":"fractal"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"034-onerule.html#term-034-onerule-2","label":"34","podNum":"34"},{"href":"035-simplestnumbers.html#term-035-simplestnumbers-4","label":"35","podNum":"35"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"iteration","term":"iteration"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"035-simplestnumbers.html#term-035-simplestnumbers-1","label":"35","podNum":"35"},{"href":"038-consonance-map.html#term-038-consonance-map-6","label":"38","podNum":"38"},{"href":"071-beauty.html","label":"71","podNum":"71"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"mathematics","term":"mathematics"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"035-simplestnumbers.html#term-035-simplestnumbers-3","label":"35","podNum":"35"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"benfords-law-benfords-law-tbd","term":"Benfords Law, benfords-law, TBD"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"035-simplestnumbers.html#term-035-simplestnumbers-5","label":"35","podNum":"35"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"virtual-dimension-formula","term":"virtual-dimension-formula"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"037-assembly.html#term-037-assembly-3","label":"37","podNum":"37"}],"definition":"Walker et al.: complexity measured by assembly index (steps to construct); high-index, high-copy objects signal life."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"assembly-theory","term":"Assembly theory"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"038-consonance-map.html","label":"38","podNum":"38"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"mandelbrot","term":"mandelbrot"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"038-consonance-map.html#term-038-consonance-map-1","label":"38","podNum":"38"}],"definition":"a single-stringed instrument used by Pythagoras to demonstrate the relationship between string length and musical pitch. Stopping the string at 1\/2 its length gives the octave, 2\/3 gives the fifth, 3\/4 gives the fourth."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"monochord","term":"monochord"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"038-consonance-map.html#term-038-consonance-map-4","label":"38","podNum":"38"},{"href":"066-cosmicattractor.html","label":"66","podNum":"66"}],"definition":"a value or region toward which a repeated mathematical process converges. The Mandelbrot set is a map of which starting points are attracted to stability and which escape to infinity."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"attractor","term":"attractor"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"038-consonance-map.html#term-038-consonance-map-5","label":"38","podNum":"38"}],"definition":"a measure of how harmonious a musical interval sounds, based on the simplicity of its frequency ratio. Simpler ratios, smaller integers, score higher."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"consonance-score","term":"consonance score"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"039-devil.html#term-039-devil-1","label":"39","podNum":"39"}],"definition":"the seventh harmonic of a vibrating object, ratio 7:4 above the fundamental. About 31 cents flatter than the equal-tempered minor seventh, and dramatically flatter than the major seventh. Naturally produced by any vibrating physical body, but unavailable to the Western 12-tone scale because no combination of integer powers of 2 and 3 (or even 2, 3, and 5) reaches a factor of 7."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"harmonic-seventh","term":"harmonic seventh"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"039-devil.html#term-039-devil-2","label":"39","podNum":"39"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"devils-interval-diabolus-in-musica-the-tritone-augmented-fourth-or-diminished-fifth-half-an-octave--called-diabolus-in-musica-in-medieval-and-renaissance-music-theory-and-avoided-in-plainchant-the-term-refers-to-the-intervals-instability--it-does-not-resolve-naturally-to-anything--not-to-any-moral-judgment-about-evil-the-myth-that-the-church-considered-it-satanic-is-a-later-embellishment","term":"devils interval, diabolus in musica, \"the tritone (augmented fourth or diminished fifth, half an octave), called \"diabolus in musica\" in medieval and Renaissance music theory and avoided in plainchant. The term refers to the intervals instability — it does not resolve naturally to anything — not to any moral judgment about evil. The myth that the church considered it Satanic is a later embellishment."},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"039-devil.html#term-039-devil-3","label":"39","podNum":"39"}],"definition":"the tiny irrational ratio, approximately 1.0136 or 23.46 cents, by which twelve perfect fifths exceed seven octaves. The mathematical obstruction to closing the circle of fifths with pure 3:2 ratios. Made musically manifest as the \"wolf fifth\" in any keyboard tuning system that uses pure fifths in most keys and concentrates the comma in one position."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"pythagorean-comma","term":"Pythagorean comma"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"040-stability.html","label":"40","podNum":"40"}],"definition":"resistance to being captured into resonant lock; irrational ratios (e.g. golden ratio) are most stable in this sense."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"dynamic-stability","term":"dynamic stability"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"040-stability.html","label":"40","podNum":"40"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"resonance","term":"resonance"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"040-stability.html","label":"40","podNum":"40"}],"definition":"A ratio that produces a short, repeating composite waveform; simple integer ratios are most stable here."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"acoustic-stability","term":"Acoustic stability"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"040-stability.html#term-040-stability-1","label":"40","podNum":"40"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"golden-ratio","term":"golden-ratio"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"041-colorwheel.html","label":"41","podNum":"41"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"color","term":"color"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"041-colorwheel.html#term-041-colorwheel-1","label":"41","podNum":"41"}],"definition":"the transformation performed by the second layer of the retina, recombining the three raw cone signals into two opponent channels, red vs green and blue vs yellow, before any signal leaves the eye."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"opponent-processing","term":"opponent processing"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"041-colorwheel.html#term-041-colorwheel-2","label":"41","podNum":"41"}],"definition":"the four perceptual primaries delivered by opponent processing, red, yellow, green, blue, which feel irreducible to any mixture. No color appears both reddish and greenish, or both bluish and yellowish."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"unique-hues","term":"unique hues"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"041-colorwheel.html#term-041-colorwheel-3","label":"41","podNum":"41"}],"definition":"mixing light of different wavelengths, where combining red and green produces yellow. The primary colors at this level are red, green, and blue."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"additive-color-mixing","term":"additive color mixing"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"041-colorwheel.html#term-041-colorwheel-4","label":"41","podNum":"41"}],"definition":"mixing pigments, where each pigment absorbs certain wavelengths. The primary colors at this level are closer to cyan, magenta, and yellow. The painter's intuition of red, yellow, blue is an approximation of this layer combined with the opponent channel layer above it."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"subtractive-color-mixing","term":"subtractive color mixing"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"041-colorwheel.html#term-041-colorwheel-6","label":"41","podNum":"41"}],"definition":"the perceptual ability to see a surface as having a stable color across different lighting conditions. Believed to be a primary function of the blue-yellow opponent channel."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"color-constancy","term":"color constancy"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"042-eyethinks.html","label":"42","podNum":"42"}],"definition":"TBD"}],"explainers":[],"slug":"retina","term":"retina"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"042-eyethinks.html","label":"42","podNum":"42"}],"definition":"recombination of cone signals into red\/green, blue\/yellow, and light\/dark channels within the retina."}],"explainers":[],"slug":"opponent-process","term":"opponent process"},{"definitions":[{"backrefs":[{"href":"044-ai-retina.html#term-044-ai-retina-1","label":"44","podNum":"44"}],"definition":"sensory systems are structured to optimally compress the statistics of natural inputs; <script>(function(){
```
function esc(s){var d=document.createElement("div");d.textContent=s==null?"":String(s);return d.innerHTML;}
function podNumKey(pn){var n=parseInt(String(pn),10);return isNaN(n)?999999:n;}
var __tn=(function(){var el=document.getElementById("gossamer-toc-numbers");if(!el)return{};try{return JSON.parse(el.textContent)||{};}catch(x){return{};}})();
function tnArr(pn){var t=__tn[pn]||String(pn);return String(t).split(".").map(function(x){return parseInt(x,10)||0;});}
function tnCmp(a,b){var x=tnArr(a),y=tnArr(b),n=Math.max(x.length,y.length);for(var i=0;i<n;i++){var d=(x[i]||0)-(y[i]||0);if(d)return d;}return 0;}
function backChip(b){var lb=__tn[b.podNum]||((b.label!=null&&b.label!=="")?b.label:b.podNum);return '<a class="gloss-backref nav-push" href="'+esc(b.href)+'">'+esc(lb)+'</a>';}
function chips(brs){return (brs||[]).map(backChip).join(" ");}
function defBlock(d){
  return '<div class="gloss-def-block"><span class="gloss-def">'+esc(d.definition)+'</span> '+chips(d.backrefs)+'</div>';
}
function explainers(exs){
  return (exs||[]).map(function(x){
    var notes=x.notes?(" "+esc(x.notes)):"";
    if(x.url)return '<div class="gloss-explainer"><a class="gloss-explainer-link" href="'+esc(x.url)+'" rel="noopener">Explainer</a>'+notes+'</div>';
    return x.notes?'<div class="gloss-explainer gloss-explainer-note">'+esc(x.notes)+'</div>':'';
  }).join("");
}
function termEntry(e){
  var defs=(e.definitions||[]).map(defBlock).join("");
  var body=defs?'<div class="gloss-defs">'+defs+'</div>':'';
  return '<div class="gloss-entry" id="'+esc(e.slug)+'">'+
    '<div class="gloss-term-line"><strong class="gloss-term">'+esc(e.term)+'</strong></div>'+
    body+explainers(e.explainers)+'</div>';
}
function flatRow(e,d,b){
  // "By number" mode mirrors the bibliography's by-number row (shared .bib-row layout):
  // chapter number on the left, then the entry. Term bold, then its definition.
  var lb=__tn[b.podNum]||((b.label!=null&&b.label!=="")?b.label:b.podNum);
  var def=(d.definition&&d.definition!=="TBD")?(' '+esc(d.definition)):'';
  return '<div class="bib-row">'+
    '<a class="bib-ref-num nav-push" href="'+esc(b.href)+'">'+esc(lb)+'</a>'+
    '<span class="bib-cite"><strong class="gloss-term">'+esc(e.term)+'</strong>'+def+'</span>'+
  '</div>';
}
function parseG(){var el=document.getElementById("gossamer-gloss-json");if(!el)return[];try{return JSON.parse(el.textContent);}catch(x){return[];}}
function renderG(mode){
  var data=parseG();
  var root=document.getElementById("gloss-entries-root");
  if(!root)return;
  if(mode==="pod"){
    var flat=[];
    data.forEach(function(e,ei){(e.definitions||[]).forEach(function(d,di){(d.backrefs||[]).forEach(function(b,bi){flat.push({e:e,d:d,b:b,ei:ei,di:di,bi:bi});});});});
    flat.sort(function(a,b){var c=tnCmp(a.b.podNum,b.b.podNum);if(c!==0)return c;if(a.ei!==b.ei)return a.ei-b.ei;if(a.di!==b.di)return a.di-b.di;return a.bi-b.bi;});
    root.innerHTML=flat.map(function(r){return flatRow(r.e,r.d,r.b);}).join("");
    return;
  }
  var sorted=data.slice().sort(function(a,b){return String(a.term).toLowerCase().localeCompare(String(b.term).toLowerCase());});
  root.innerHTML=sorted.map(termEntry).join("");
}
function init(){
  var btns=document.querySelectorAll(".gloss-sort-toggle .sort-btn");
  var K="gloss-sort-v3";
  var sv=localStorage.getItem(K);
  if(sv!=="term"&&sv!=="pod")sv="term";
```

The @term(field, fields, "in physics, an entity that has a value at every point in space. Not a stage on which events occur but the very medium through which events reach out and feel each other. chapters/013-enactment.md:15: @image(013-enactment,Organism enacting its world through active engagement,Wikimedia Commons) A bacterium swimming toward a chemical gradient is not merely following a rule. chapters/039-devil.md-7- @argument
chapters/039-devil.md:8: The label "devil's interval" began in medieval music theory as a warning about a specific dissonant interval, but the cultural pattern it names — calling unfamiliar harmonics evil because the framework cannot accommodate them — has been ported across centuries onto every tradition that bent notes off the equal-tempered grid, and the irony is that the bending almost always leads not toward dissonance but toward consonances simpler and older than anything the keyboard can reach.

chapters/039-devil.md-67- Diabolus in musica.

chapters/039-devil.md-69- The actual history is duller and more interesting.
chapters/039-devil.md:70: Plainchant avoided the tritone because it does not resolve, not because anyone thought the devil lived in it.
chapters/039-devil.md-71- The "diabolus" label appears in 18th-century counterpoint treatises as a mnemonic for student composers: this interval is unstable, treat it carefully.
--
chapters/039-devil.md-169- Plainchant avoided it because it does not resolve.

chapters/039-devil.md:188: "Devil's interval" was never used in this way directly — the term remained attached to the tritone — but the shape of the labeling, the willingness to call unfamiliar consonance evil because the framework could not accommodate it, has run continuously through Western musical reception for centuries.
chapters/039-devil.md-189- Whether or not the specific term migrated, the cultural pattern it names has done its work elsewhere under other names.

New server has a lot of new features, including this message using the "broadcast" feature and:

* The browse navigation bar is much cleaner
* The project map works much better
* Projects can now include an image + brief summary
* Public projects show that info on the gossamerapp.com home page

And you can now:
* Export to PDF as well as Zip/Markdown/Word
* Export to Word and PDF is improved with image captions, live hyperlinks, Resources section, etc.
* Log into gossamerapp.com to manage your username and projects, including adding collaborators
* Make an invite in the app to let you set up a passkey that works in your browser

```
## Possible new trail: "The Crack That Lets the Light In"

- Trade-off: every pod lives on exactly one trail, so pulling these would leave gaps in existing trails. Worth mapping which trails currently claim them before committing.
```

@chip(replace-from, 22-16-16.639, 2026-07-03.22-16-16.639)
@title Gossamer App Test
@edit-time 2026-05-07T21:18Z
A framework for collections of linked ideas
@status proto
@pub-time 2026-03-27T00:00Z
@tags meta, framework, software, collections

Gossamer is an iOS/macOS app for building and exploring collections of linked ideas, with an AI collaborator to help think things through.
SeedPods is one such collection.
Yours might be something else entirely.

## What a Collection Is

A collection is a directory of markdown files:
- `content/pods/` — the pods themselves
- `content/config/` — settings, categories, styling
- `content/about/`, `content/more/`, etc. — supporting pages
- `content/internal/` — documentation

Each pod has metadata fields (#title, #subtitle, #status, #date, #category, #tags), up to six content layers (surface, depth, provenance, script, images, brief), 
and links to other pods via \@link(NNN) syntax.

## The AI Collaborator

Claude has access to the full archive index, can read any file on request, and can propose edits via structured edit blocks.
Edits are applied silently — Claude proposes, the user accepts, the file is written.
Claude receives no confirmation; it learns the edit succeeded when the user sends their next message.

## To-Do

- [ ] **Bug: `field` + `mode: set` replaces entire file**: When Claude uses the edit protocol with `field: title` and `mode: set`, the app appears to replace the entire file with just the block content, rather than surgically replacing only that metadata line. Workaround: use `mode: patch` with find/replace for metadata changes. The system prompt or edit protocol documentation should clarify this — or the app behavior should be fixed.
- [ ] **UI: nav bar should be sticky**: The page navigation bar scrolls away with content. It should remain fixed at the top.
- [ ] **UI: translucent blur behind top panels**: Both the chat pane and browse pane should have a translucent panel with blur effect behind the top info area, so scrolling content is visible but muted beneath it.
- [x] **BROWSE directive working**: Opens as a bottom sheet that can be dismissed by swiping down. Clean interaction.
- [ ] **Bug: build cache not always invalidating**: After an edit is accepted, the built HTML sometimes doesn't update until app restart. "Force rebuild" in settings gives no feedback. The build should either auto-trigger reliably when switching to browse, or force rebuild should confirm it ran.
- [ ] **Bug: scroll position after accepting edit**: Conversation view scrolls away after accepting. Should maintain position.
- [ ] Define generic vocabulary (what to call a pod in other collections)
- [ ] Create minimal starter template for new collections
- [ ] Document onboarding flow for new users
- [ ] Write the "first run" greeting prompt
- [ ] Specify what's SeedPods-specific vs. framework-generic
- [ ] Design collection switching UX
- [ ] Document the edit protocol for AI collaborators
- [ ] Create example collections (recipe collection? research notes? worldbuilding?)

Gossamer emerged from the SeedPods project in March 2026.
Christopher Alexander's "A Pattern Language" is the structural ancestor.

@chip(replace-from, 22-16-16.639, 2026-07-03.22-16-16.640)
@title Multi-User Collaboration — Design Spec
@argument
Design specification for the first multi-user collaboration features in Gossamer: invitation, roles, pending edits, and notification.
@status proto
@pub-time 2026-05-02T00:00Z
@related 100, 101

## Overview

Allow one user (the owner) to invite others to share and collaborate on a project. This spec covers the MVP: roles, project/user data model, pending edit review, agent behavior, and summarization. The invitation flow and notification system are deferred — initial user/project mapping is done manually via an admin screen.

## Roles

Three roles, in order of privilege:

- **Owner.** Full control. Can accept or decline edits, make direct changes. One per project (for now).
- **Contributor.** Can participate in chat, ask the agent to propose edits. Cannot accept or decline edits — proposals go to the owner for review.
- **Observer.** Can see the chat and browse the project. Cannot speak in chat (no text input window shown). Cannot propose edits.

## Data model

Two JSON files serve as a minimal database. Both live in the root of shared data and will eventually be replaced by a single SQLite database.

### projects.json

```json
[
  {
    "id": "SeedPods",
    "name": "SeedPods",
    "members": [
      { "userId": "FA2ABF2F-24EF-40A0-8D65-A0A4FFA3E4E3", "role": "owner" }
    ]
  }
]
```

Each project has an `id`, a display `name`, and a `members` array of `{ userId, role }` pairs. `userId` is currently a username; it will migrate to an opaque ID in a later pass. Roles are `owner`, `contributor`, or `observer`.

### users.json

A user registry. No project membership — that lives in `projects.json`.

The `id` field is currently a UUID token that doubles as a shared secret (future passkey). It will eventually become an opaque ID separate from authentication.

```json
[
  {
    "id": "FA2ABF2F-24EF-40A0-8D65-A0A4FFA3E4E3",
    "name": "Darakshan",
    "initials": "darak",
    "devices": [
      "F86E0D90-9B7A-4EDF-BCC0-70F721D036D9",
      "5E2DCE06-6BD4-4669-A4D9-177DEC7F31C7"
    ],
    "profile": "73 years old, experienced software architect, student of spiritual traditions and philosophy, founder of SeedPods"
  }
]
```

### Admin screen

A page in the admin interface allows the owner to:

- Add a user to a project with a role (by selecting from known users).
- Change a user's role.
- Remove a user from a project.

This replaces the full invitation flow for now. No notification badges, no accept/decline dialogue.

### Migration

The current `config/users.json` inside each project directory is retired. Its data moves to the server-level `users.json` and `projects.json`. The per-project file can remain as a read-only cache if needed for offline use, but the server-level files are authoritative.

## Project switching

Once a user has access to more than one project, they need a way to switch. Switching changes both the chat pane and the browse pane. The UI for this is TBD but could be a project selector in the settings pane or a swipe gesture.

## Pending edits

When a contributor asks the agent to make a change, the agent proposes the edit as a chip. But only the owner can accept or decline it.

- The chip enters a **pending** state. The conversation continues — it does not block.
- The contributor sees their proposal marked "waiting for owner."
- The agent knows the proposal is pending and does not treat it as applied.
- The owner can review pending edits in the browse pane. The proposed change appears as a highlighted diff overlay on the rendered subject, with accept/decline buttons in context.
- A **"next pending"** button on the chip lets the owner cycle through all pending edits.
- This is analogous to document review in Word or Google Docs, except the owner sees the change as a reader would, not as raw markup.

## Agent behavior in multi-user chat

- The agent can see all participants and their roles.
- **Subject detection:** The agent watches the conversation and matches it against subject arguments. When confident, it surfaces a relevant subject via the status line.
- **Hand-raising:** The agent can signal it has something to contribute via the status line, without interrupting.
- **Status line:** A single line the agent can write to at any time — subject detection, hand-raising, pending-edit count, reactions, emoji. Participants can react with emoji back.
- **Mute modes:** The group can toggle between lightweight mode (subject detection only) and full mode (agent follows conversation and can contribute). Muting saves cost.
- The group trains the agent over time. Ignored contributions are signal.

## Minutes (server-triggered summarization)

The server runs a configurable timer (default 30 minutes) after the last message in any user's current chat. When it fires, the server sends the agent a message asking it to summarize the conversation as a summary chip.

The summary captures three things:

- **What was established.** Things the group now holds that they didn't before.
- **What's open.** Things raised but not resolved.
- **What's next.** Anything anyone committed to doing.

These categories are domain-agnostic. The same mechanism fires when context is getting full. One feature, two triggers, same output. The summary is stored as a chip and persists across sessions.

## Notification system (deferred)

The full notification system is deferred to a later phase. It would include:

- Red dot on the lozenge for pending invitations and unread items.
- App badge when any notification is active.
- Settings sidebar highlighting the section wanting attention.
- Lozenge hides when keyboard is active, reappears when keyboard dismisses.
- Invitation flow: owner provides email, invitee gets badge, accept/decline/cancel dialogue.

## Open questions

- What happens to a contributor's pending edits if they are removed from the project?
- How does the "next pending" button order the edits — chronological, by subject, by contributor?
- Should the agent carry context from previous group sessions via stored minutes, or start fresh each time?
- Can there be multiple owners?

```
<div class="wrap"><div class="page-body home-page fade"><h1 data-src-file="content/footnotes.md" data-src-l0="1" data-src-l1="1">Footnotes</h1>
<div class="note-entries-root">
<div class="note-row" id="note-029-lighttime-1"><div class="note-head"><a class="note-num nav-push" href="029-lighttime.html#note-029-lighttime-1">4.3</a><a class="note-pod-title nav-push" href="029-lighttime.html">From Light's Point of View, There Is No Journey</a></div><div class="note-text">below from a separate conversation to be integrated:<br>Starlight is hitting you right now, even at noon on a bright sunny day. The photons from distant stars don’t stop arriving just because the sun is up. What changes is the signal-to-noise ratio, not the signal. The sun floods your retinas and the atmosphere with so much visible light that the comparatively faint stellar photons are simply drowned out, your eyes can’t detect the contrast. But the photons are physically impinging on you all the same.<br>A few layers to this:<br>The atmosphere scatters sunlight in all directions. This is why the daytime sky is bright blue rather than black, Rayleigh scattering fills the whole sky dome with scattered solar photons. That diffuse brightness is what makes stars invisible in the daytime, not any absence of starlight.<br>From space or at high altitude, stars are visible in daytime. Astronauts on the ISS, if they shade their eyes from direct sunlight, can see stars at any hour. On very high mountains the sky is a deeper blue for the same reason, less atmosphere to scatter.<br>Radio telescopes observe stars 24/7. At wavelengths where the sun isn’t overwhelming, astronomers don’t distinguish day from night at all. The electromagnetic radiation from distant stars arrives continuously.<br>Your skin is also receiving it. Not just your eyes, every square centimeter of your body is being struck by photons from thousands of stars simultaneously, all the time, day or night, clouds or clear. The flux is just extraordinarily low compared to solar radiation.<br>So the starlight is never not shining on you. The sun’s presence is an observational obstacle, not a physical one.</div></div>
<div class="note-row" id="note-020-penrose-1"><div class="note-head"><a class="note-num nav-push" href="020-penrose.html#note-020-penrose-1">6.6</a><a class="note-pod-title nav-push" href="020-penrose.html">The Arrow Points Somewhere Else</a></div><div class="note-text"><a class="bib-ref" id="ref-footnotes-1" href="bibliography.html#ref-footnotes-1">[penrose]</a>
The original argument, Penrose uses Gödel to argue that human mathematical understanding is non-algorithmic.
For the extended defense against objections, see <a class="bib-ref" id="ref-footnotes-2" href="bibliography.html#ref-footnotes-2">[penrose-2]</a></div></div>
</div>
</div></div>
```

@bubble(agent, 2026-07-03.22-31-54.431)
That's a great long-term goal. A well-crafted transition adds a lot — it's the connective tissue that makes a sequence feel like one thought moving forward rather than a set of separate essays.

A couple of things worth thinking about before doing the work:

1. **Order first, transitions second.** If some sections aren't in their best order, writing transitions locks in the current order — you'd have to redo them if you resequence. Better to first ask: within each section, is the current sequence right? Only then write the transitions.

2. **Transition is a to-position, not a chapter.** A transition belongs to the *pair* — how X ends up leading to Y. If Y moves, the transition to it moves too. So the transition section really lives at the end of chapter X (as it currently does), but its content depends entirely on what Y is.

3. **What makes a good transition?** A few models worth trying out:
   - **Question-forward**: end with a question the next chapter answers
   - **Concept-forward**: introduce a term or move that the next chapter takes seriously
   - **Metaphor-forward**: seed an image the next chapter picks up
   - **Contrast-forward**: name what the current chapter *didn't* address, to be picked up next

If you want, we could:
- (a) start by auditing one section's order — Knowledge, say, since it's the opening arc and matters most
- (b) look at the one chapter that already has a transition section and see what shape you want the pattern to take
- (c) pick a chapter you know is well-placed and draft a transition together, to establish the voice

Which one appeals?

@bubble(agent, 2026-07-03.22-33-42.370)
Three transitions, three different shapes. Let me lay them out:

**001-caloric → 027-usedtothink** (long, argumentative)
Recaps the current chapter's move (frameworks dissolving), names the *hidden cost* (phenomenon lost with mechanism), gives a for-instance (inside of things), then hands off with "@link(027) picks up exactly there." Six or seven sentences. It's essentially a short essay.

**039-devil → multiple** (a fan-out)
Recognizes the current chapter's shape ("the framework discards the mechanism and discards the phenomenon with it"), then names three or four downstream chapters and what each does with the thread. Not a single-direction bridge but a hub telling you what forks from here.

**055-patience → 069-love** (compressed, poetic)
Two sentences. "Patience builds the reflectors. @link(069) takes up what happens when two of them meet." Almost imagistic. Doesn't recap or argue — just points.

Three real patterns, and they're all authentic to the chapters they end. The 001 one is patient because the whole book has to earn its first move; the 039 one is a hub because 039 sits at a junction of arguments; the 055 one is tiny because 055 has already said what it needed to and doesn't want to over-explain.

**A tentative principle:** the transition should have the same *density and voice* as the chapter it ends. Don't force a uniform format. What should be uniform:

1. It's a real section (`@section(transition)`), not just a trailing paragraph.
2. It names *at least one* downstream chapter by `@link(NNN)` — the primary next.
3. It gives a reason, however brief, why the next chapter follows from this one. Not a summary, a *seam*.

**What's specifically nice in the three examples:**
- 001's "we rarely check whether it was also holding something the new map doesn't have a name for yet" — that's the *seam sentence*. If you can name what was lost, or what was seeded, or what's about to be tested, the transition works.
- 039's fan-out — this is worth naming as its own category. Some chapters really are hubs; they should point in multiple directions with a light touch, not force a single next.
- 055's tightness — this is where I'd point new transitions toward, unless the chapter genuinely needs more.

**Suggested shape going forward** (three types, pick per chapter):

- **Bridge** (default, 2–4 sentences): one seam sentence + one link + one why-this-follows sentence.
- **Fan** (hub chapters): shorter seam + 2–4 links each with one clause of orientation.
- **Question** (chapters that end open): pose the question the next chapter answers, link it, done.

Want me to draft a transition for one specific chapter as a test? Pick a pair (X ends, Y begins) where you're confident about the ordering, and I'll try one in each shape so we can see which feels closest to voice.

Fair. Withdrawn — my "match the chapter's density and voice" advice was reading polish where there's just work-in-progress. The real principle is simpler:

**A transition is a seam sentence + a link.** Once you're doing the final consistency pass, the transitions can match whatever voice you settle on for the whole book. Until then, they can all follow the same simple shape and be swapped out later.

Working shape:

1. One sentence that names *what this chapter leaves on the table* — the loose thread, the question left open, the concept introduced that needs testing.
2. `@link(NNN)` with a phrase, one clause, saying what the next chapter does with it.
3. Optional third: a second link if the chapter genuinely hubs to more than one place.

That's it. No summary of what just happened, no "we saw that…", no formal essay. Just: what's not yet resolved, and where it goes next.

When you're ready to start writing them, I can draft in that shape for any pair. Or if you're not ready yet — because you're right, ordering is upstream of transitions — we can leave this and pick it up when you are. What would you like?

As a meta-comment, you used to do a lot of stuttering and now you're down to two. I wonder if the second prompt from your harness, when you forget to wish you a command, is effective and should be the first prompt.
@clue(route, 22-43-28.677, collaborator)
@bubble(agent, 2026-07-03.22-43-29.059)
On the stuttering — good observation. The "you didn't call a tool" nudge does seem to shake me out of it. The reason it works, I think, is that it forces the *action* to happen in the same reply as the intention. When I start a reply with "Let me check X..." and then don't call the tool, I've *described* the action without performing it, and the harness has to prompt me again to complete it. The nudge collapses that gap.

Whether it should be the *first* prompt: I'd guess yes for many models, no for some. Making "describe what you'll do AND do it in the same reply" the default framing would probably help most agents most of the time. But there's a small risk: for genuinely conversational turns where no action is needed, a prompt that heavily emphasizes "call tools immediately" could push the agent to fake tool calls (search when the user just wanted to chat, edit when the user was thinking out loud). The current shape — permissive default, corrective nudge when needed — has the virtue that it doesn't degrade the pure-conversation case.

A middle path: the first prompt could add one sentence like *"When you say you'll do X, do X in the same reply — don't split intent from action."* That's the specific rule the second prompt teaches; frontloading it might catch the issue earlier without pushing toward over-tooling.

Worth passing to whoever maintains the prompt. Want me to file it as feedback somewhere, or is this just an observation?
